2026-08-29T19:15:18Z  NOTE  OPERATOR QUESTION 'who votes to remove responsabile 0 at the beginning' produced a SPEC finding, derived empirically against the accepted core rather than reasoned. Answer: only Anna. Base threshold is bgMajority = floor((adminCount+1)/2); at bootstrap she is the sole admin so the threshold is 1, and the cited theorem proposer_mem_approvals counts the proposer among the assents, so PROPOSING IS THE VOTE — verified: self-demotion proposal enacts immediately and yields credit {kind:removeResponsabile,key:anna}. Degenerate but arguably unavoidable at n=1. THE REAL FINDING IS n=2: floor((2+1)/2)=1, so with exactly two responsabili either can UNILATERALLY demote the other with no assent from the peer. Verified empirically: anna alone proposed demoting bruno and it ENACTED, producing a removeResponsabile credit. Threshold only becomes a genuine constraint at n>=3. CHECKED FIDELITY BEFORE CALLING IT A BUG: lean/KelGroups/State.lean:32 defines majority as (adminCount gs + 1) / 2 and lean/KelGroups/Tests.lean:86 explicitly asserts majorityTwo == 1, so the simulator is FAITHFUL and this is a specification/design issue, not a transcription defect. Consequence for the proof surface: baseEnacted_threshold_met proves the threshold was met, but at n=2 'threshold met' means one person — the proposer — assented, which is the exact class of true-theorem-over-weak-definition hazard I have been fencing, now concrete rather than hypothetical. This is the operator's V-2 territory and belongs with the queued threshold questions.

2026-08-29T19:28:34Z  NOTE  OPERATOR FOUND A REAL TIMING BUG in the teaching strips, sharper than my NOTE-032 copy diagnosis: 'messages have to appear only when they apply — where is the negative cassa now?'. VERIFIED IN SOURCE: teachStrip('cassa') renders on every visit to the Cassiere tab regardless of balance, while line 2554 'if (tag === closePurchase) { teachLearn(cassa); teachLearn(chiusa); }' RETIRES it permanently — and closePurchase is the event where the cassiere pays the supplier, i.e. THE EVENT THAT CREATES THE NEGATIVE CASSA. So the first time a user ever sees a negative cassa the explanation is switched off in the same instant, leaving a negative number with no explanation, which is the exact situation 'ha pagato il fornitore. Non e un errore' was written for. Shown when it does not apply; retired the moment it does. ROOT CAUSE: the trigger is a VIEW (teachStrip called inside a panel's HTML) and the retirement is an EVENT (teachLearn on an applied tag) — neither is the CONDITION the message is about. RULE COMMISSIONED (NOTE-033 amending NOTE-032): show while the condition actually holds (cassa<0, question really open, escrow really held, action really refused, purchase really gone); retire on comprehension via the dismiss or repeated exposure, NEVER on the event that causes the phenomenon; benvenuto is the one legitimate view-triggered exception and must be declared as such. Required per-strip demonstration of the show-condition and retire-condition proven on the real page (reach the state and show it visible, leave the state and show it gone, dismiss and show it stays gone), with the cassa case written as a FAILING regression check first — close a purchase, cassa goes negative, explanation on screen at that moment — mirroring the operator's election scenario, since today the page does the exact opposite. My NOTE-032 register diagnosis stands but was only half: correct copy fired at the wrong moment is still a defect, and both halves ship together.

Invariants seeded from the Lean's proved laws plus the four in EXPECTED-TRACES, with solvency explicitly restricted to reachable states so we do not assert what the Lean does not. CRUCIAL FRAMING GIVEN: these are properties the Lean PROVES, so a simulator counterexample is a TRANSCRIPTION DIVERGENCE between JS and Lean — the one class of defect proofs cannot catch and the whole reason the tool is worth building — to be reported as such and not as a model bug until the Lean itself is checked. Haskell research scoped as report-only, no code: Hedgehog vs QuickCheck with integrated shrinking as the deciding factor, hedgehog state-machine support and quickcheck-state-machine against the fact that our model IS a command state machine with pre/postconditions, whether Haskell can derive its command set from the same pinned Lean surface or would hand-maintain one and thereby reintroduce the table-versus-table defect in a second language, and the cost of both languages consuming and emitting the same v1 envelope. Required the library choice ARGUED, not asserted.

2026-09-05T05:00:20Z  NOTE  TWO OPERATOR RULINGS TAKEN, recorded at questions/A-V2-AND-PLEDGE-AGENCY.md. (1) V-2 THRESHOLD: the PROPOSER DOES NOT COUNT as an assent — a proposal opens at zero. The arithmetic (adminCount+1)/2 is explicitly NOT changing and floor(n/2)+1 was offered and DECLINED; what changes is that the proposer no longer supplies one of the required assents, so every decision above n=1 needs agreement from someone who did not propose it and the n=2 unilateral-promotion anomaly disappears as a CONSEQUENCE rather than a special case. This also aligns the base channel with the vote machine, which already opens questions with empty tallies and does not credit the proposer (applyVoteEvent, Vote/Fold.lean:77, annotated 'divergenza deliberata dal legacy') — the two channels stop disagreeing about what proposing means, which was the substantive incoherence behind the operator's original 'conteggi VUOTI' observation. (2) PLEDGE AGENCY: free while PENDING, referente required after ACCEPTANCE — before acceptance the money is still the member's and nobody has relied on it; after acceptance the referente may have committed to the supplier and a unilateral withdrawal strands them. Consequences: pledge must authorize signer==u (it currently demands isResponsabile view signer, so a member cannot pledge for themselves AT ALL — #48's central ruling, never landed); correctPledge becomes TWO REGIMES, signer==u for col.pending with v'=0 as withdrawal and the existing referente authorization retained for col.accepted; solvency guard and closePurchase unchanged. REQUIRED OF THE UI: the two regimes must be legible BEFORE they matter — a member told 'you can change this' and later 'you cannot' without knowing why is the two-stage election flow repeating. NEITHER RULING IS AUTHORIZED FOR ANTICIPATION: they land as their own slices against current master with fresh audits, and until then the simulator keeps transcribing the Lean verbatim — the oracle pinning bgMajority at n=2 to 1 stays correct and must not be softened in advance.
